# CrowdStrike Security Inspector

Portable contract for the shipped CrowdStrike Falcon prevention policy, response readiness, device control and firewall, sensor coverage, and access governance assessments over the CrowdStrike Falcon API.

## Purpose

CrowdStrike Security Inspector gives auditors a read-only view of a CrowdStrike Falcon tenant. It covers five areas:

- Prevention: whether assigned prevention policies enable machine learning, exploit mitigation, script control, sensor tamper protection, and on-write detection.
- Response: whether response policies allow Real Time Response with bounded sessions, and whether alerts are triaged and hosts contained in time.
- Device control and firewall: whether device control and host firewall policies enforce.
- Sensor coverage: whether sensors update automatically and cover every host, host group, and discovered asset with an acceptable Zero Trust score.
- Access governance: who administers the tenant, which API clients can write to sensitive collections, which exclusions weaken detection, and whether Identity Protection has an active enforcing rule.

## Rationale

Falcon answers each API client only for the collections its scopes grant, and modules such as Identity Protection, Discover, and Zero Trust Assessment exist only on licensed tenants. A portable implementation must keep a refusal distinct from an empty answer. A 401, 403, or 404 from a module reports that module as unlicensed or not permitted, never as compliant.

Most lists come from offset walks and ID lookups that stop at a cap, and a few come from a single ID read whose total can exceed what it returned. Each of those conditions marks the list partial and caps the checks built on it at warn. A fail that rests on something being absent from a partial list becomes manual instead. The public API documents no last-used date for API clients, so when a client does return one, its staleness reaches evidence only, never the verdict.

## Runtime behavior

- The tool only reads. Besides the OAuth2 token request, its only POST requests are the user lookup and the alert search, which change nothing.
- Policy lists stop at 5000 policies; hosts stop at host_limit (5000), alerts at alert_limit (2000), users and API clients at user_limit (500), each exclusion type at exclusion_limit (500), and each user's role grants at 500.
- A refused or failed read never counts as an empty list. The checks that need it are manual and name the read, and a read that depends on a failed one is recorded as not collected.
- Exclusion regexes, paths, names, descriptions, and comments pass through the data redaction pass and the exclusion text pass before they reach evidence, summaries, decision facts, or the bundle.
- The 90-day periods for stale admin logins and unused API clients are fixed and cannot be configured.

## Non-goals

- Changing Falcon settings, containing hosts, opening Real Time Response sessions, or issuing any administrative write request
- Judging detection content, threat hunting results, or incident handling quality
- Reconciling the host inventory against an authoritative source such as a CMDB
- Treating a refused or unlicensed module as a compliant one
- Reproducing a particular programming language, package layout, or command-line framework

## Portable implementation guidance

Keep the API client, pagination, evidence projection, verdict evaluation, and bundle writer as separable concerns. Count from the full collected arrays at the decision point rather than from evidence previews. Keep each dataset's read state (read, refused, not collected because an earlier read failed, or partial) next to its records so every finding can say which sources it depended on and why a pass was capped.

## Shared integration contract

This specification requires [shared integration contract version 1.1](./integration-contract.md). The raw contract is available at https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ethanolivertroy/grclanker/main/specs/integration-contract.md.

## Known runtime gaps

- A user the UUID query lists but the user details lookup, POST /user-management/entities/users/GET/v1, does not return (deleted or hidden between the two reads) is left out of CS-16 and CS-17 without marking the user inventory partial, so both checks can pass on the users the lookup returned.

## Tools

| Tool | Purpose | Finding IDs | Result shape |
|---|---|---|---|
| `crowdstrike_check_access` | Validate read-only Falcon API access across prevention, response, device control, firewall, sensor update, hosts, host groups, users, API clients, Discover, alerts, exclusions, Zero Trust Assessment, Identity Protection, and RTR audit surfaces, and report missing API client scopes. | None | A text result whose structured details preserve the runtime assessment or access-check object byte-for-byte. |
| `crowdstrike_assess_prevention_policies` | Assess Falcon prevention policies for CS-01 through CS-05: ML detection and prevention slider levels, exploit mitigation toggles, script-based execution control, sensor tampering protection, and on-write detection and quarantine. Only enabled policies assigned to host groups (or platform_default) count as enforcement. | `CS-01`, `CS-02`, `CS-03`, `CS-04`, `CS-05` | A text result whose structured details preserve the runtime assessment or access-check object byte-for-byte. |
| `crowdstrike_assess_response_readiness` | Assess Falcon response readiness for CS-06, CS-07, CS-22, and CS-23: Real Time Response enablement and custom script restrictions, RTR session limits (manual confirmation with audit evidence), critical/high alert response SLA, and network containment documentation. | `CS-06`, `CS-07`, `CS-22`, `CS-23` | A text result whose structured details preserve the runtime assessment or access-check object byte-for-byte. |
| `crowdstrike_assess_device_firewall` | Assess Falcon device control and host firewall posture for CS-08 through CS-11: USB mass storage blocking and exceptions, Bluetooth and PCIe/Thunderbolt restrictions, firewall policy enforcement with active rule groups, and default inbound DENY with documented allow rules. | `CS-08`, `CS-09`, `CS-10`, `CS-11` | A text result whose structured details preserve the runtime assessment or access-check object byte-for-byte. |
| `crowdstrike_assess_sensor_coverage` | Assess Falcon sensor coverage for CS-12 through CS-15 and CS-25: sensor update auto-update or N-2 pinning with uninstall protection, deployment completeness by last-seen age, host group assignment coverage, Falcon Discover unmanaged assets, and Zero Trust Assessment scores below threshold. | `CS-12`, `CS-13`, `CS-14`, `CS-15`, `CS-25` | A text result whose structured details preserve the runtime assessment or access-check object byte-for-byte. |
| `crowdstrike_assess_access_governance` | Assess Falcon RBAC, API client, exclusion, and identity protection posture for CS-16 through CS-21 and CS-24: admin count and shared accounts, least privilege and stale admins, API client write scopes, IOA/ML/sensor visibility exclusion review, and Identity Protection enforcement. | `CS-16`, `CS-17`, `CS-18`, `CS-19`, `CS-20`, `CS-21`, `CS-24` | A text result whose structured details preserve the runtime assessment or access-check object byte-for-byte. |
| `crowdstrike_export_audit_bundle` | Export a CrowdStrike Falcon audit package covering all 25 spec controls: projected and redacted API snapshots (with not-collected markers for denied datasets), normalized findings, executive summary, unified compliance matrix, per-framework reports (FedRAMP, CMMC, SOC 2, CIS, PCI-DSS, DISA STIG, IRAP, ISMAP), quick reference, error log, and a zip archive named after the allocated bundle directory. | `CS-01`, `CS-02`, `CS-03`, `CS-04`, `CS-05`, `CS-06`, `CS-07`, `CS-22`, `CS-23`, `CS-08`, `CS-09`, `CS-10`, `CS-11`, `CS-12`, `CS-13`, `CS-14`, `CS-15`, `CS-25`, `CS-16`, `CS-17`, `CS-18`, `CS-19`, `CS-20`, `CS-21`, `CS-24` | A text result plus output directory, paired archive path, file count, finding count, and collection-error count. |

### Parameters

#### `crowdstrike_check_access`

| Parameter | Kind | Required | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `client_id` | string | no | Falcon API client ID. Defaults to CS_CLIENT_ID (or FALCON_CLIENT_ID), then ~/.crowdstrike/config.json. |
| `client_secret` | string | no | Falcon API client secret. Defaults to CS_CLIENT_SECRET (or FALCON_CLIENT_SECRET), then the config file. |
| `base_url` | string | no | Falcon API base URL. Defaults to CS_BASE_URL, then the cloud alias, then https://api.crowdstrike.com. |
| `cloud` | string | no | Falcon cloud alias: us-1, us-2, eu-1, us-gov-1, or us-gov-2. Defaults to CS_CLOUD. Ignored when base_url is set. |
| `member_cid` | string | no | Optional child CID for Flight Control (MSSP) tenants. Defaults to CS_MEMBER_CID. Results cover that CID only. |
| `config_file` | string | no | Optional JSON config file with client_id, client_secret, base_url, cloud, and member_cid. Defaults to CS_CONFIG_FILE or ~/.crowdstrike/config.json. |
| `timeout_seconds` | number | no | HTTP timeout in seconds. Defaults to 30. |

## Authentication

Supported modes:

- OAuth2 client credentials: client_id and client_secret are exchanged for a bearer token, sent as Authorization: Bearer on every API read

Credential precedence, highest first:

1. Explicit tool arguments
2. CS_* environment variables, then their FALCON_* aliases
3. CrowdStrike JSON config file
4. A base_url from any source takes precedence over a cloud from any source; with neither, the us-1 API host is used

Environment variables: `CS_CONFIG_FILE`, `CS_CLIENT_ID`, `FALCON_CLIENT_ID`, `CS_CLIENT_SECRET`, `FALCON_CLIENT_SECRET`, `CS_CLOUD`, `FALCON_CLOUD`, `CS_BASE_URL`, `FALCON_BASE_URL`, `CS_MEMBER_CID`, `FALCON_MEMBER_CID`, `CS_TIMEOUT`

Configuration locations: ~/.crowdstrike/config.json, Explicit path from config_file or CS_CONFIG_FILE

Credential and deployment variants: Named clouds us-1 (the default, https://api.crowdstrike.com), us-2, eu-1, us-gov-1, and us-gov-2, each with its own API host, Cloud aliases us1, us2, eu1, usgov1, usgov2, gov-1, gov, and laggar, matched case-insensitively with underscores read as hyphens, An explicit https base_url (the scheme may be omitted; http is rejected) replaces the cloud's API host, and a base_url equal to a named cloud's host reports that cloud, A Flight Control child CID (member_cid) is sent only in the token request, so the token and every read made with it cover that CID only

Configuration fields: `client_id`, `clientId`, `client_secret`, `clientSecret`, `cloud`, `region`, `base_url`, `baseUrl`, `member_cid`, `memberCid`, `timeout_seconds`

Malformed configuration: Reject malformed or ambiguous configuration before any request; never echo credential values.

Credential refresh: POST {base_url}/oauth2/token with Accept: application/json and an application/x-www-form-urlencoded body of client_id, client_secret, and member_cid when configured; the access_token is reused until 60 seconds before its expires_in (1799 seconds when absent) runs out, and for at least 60 seconds.

### Request construction

| Surface | Input | Exact value or rule | Required |
|---|---|---|---|
| `prevention_policies` | client | Use the resolved API host (base_url, else the selected cloud's host); member_cid scopes the bearer token and is never sent on a read. | yes |
| `prevention_policies` | headers | Accept: application/json; Authorization: Bearer {access_token} | yes |
| `prevention_policies` | response | A JSON object whose resources array holds one prevention policy per entry and whose meta.pagination may carry total, offset, and next; a status outside 200 through 299 after retries, a body that is not a JSON object, or a missing resources array is a refusal. | yes |
| `prevention_policies` | query:limit | 500, or the policies left under the 5000 cap when fewer; 1 on the crowdstrike_check_access probe | yes |
| `prevention_policies` | query:offset | The offset: the previous page's meta.pagination.next (else meta.pagination.offset) when that is not a whole number; otherwise the previous page's offset (0 for the first page or after such a cursor) plus the number of policies it returned; On every page after the first | no |
| `response_policies` | client | Use the resolved API host (base_url, else the selected cloud's host); member_cid scopes the bearer token and is never sent on a read. | yes |
| `response_policies` | headers | Accept: application/json; Authorization: Bearer {access_token} | yes |
| `response_policies` | response | A JSON object whose resources array holds one response policy per entry and whose meta.pagination may carry total, offset, and next; a status outside 200 through 299 after retries, a body that is not a JSON object, or a missing resources array is a refusal. | yes |

## Pagination

| Surfaces | Cursor or marker | Page size | Item cap | Page cap | Total semantics | Stop conditions |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `prevention_policies`, `response_policies`, `device_control_policies`, `firewall_policies`, `sensor_update_policies` | `meta.pagination.total`, `meta.pagination.next`, `meta.pagination.offset` | service default | 5000 | none | Pages of 500 policies (limit), or the policies left under the 5000 cap when fewer. The first page sends no offset; each later page sends the previous page's meta.pagination.next (else meta.pagination.offset) when that is not a whole number; otherwise the previous page's offset (0 for the first page or after such a cursor) plus the number of policies it returned. Without meta.pagination.total the walk runs to an empty page or the cap. A returned resource that is not a JSON object is dropped and marks the list partial. | meta.pagination.total policies read; An empty page, which marks the list partial when fewer than meta.pagination.total policies were read; 5000 policies read, which marks the list partial when meta.pagination.total is larger or, without a total, when the last page was full or carried a cursor that is not a whole number; A cursor that is not a whole number and repeats an earlier page's, which marks the list partial |
| `host_groups` | `meta.pagination.total`, `meta.pagination.next`, `meta.pagination.offset` | service default | 5000 | none | Pages of 500 host groups (limit), or the host groups left under the 5000 cap when fewer. The first page sends no offset; each later page sends the previous page's meta.pagination.next (else meta.pagination.offset) when that is not a whole number; otherwise the previous page's offset (0 for the first page or after such a cursor) plus the number of host groups it returned. Without meta.pagination.total the walk runs to an empty page or the cap. A returned resource that is not a JSON object is dropped and marks the list partial. | meta.pagination.total host groups read; An empty page, which marks the list partial when fewer than meta.pagination.total host groups were read; 5000 host groups read, which marks the list partial when meta.pagination.total is larger or, without a total, when the last page was full or carried a cursor that is not a whole number; A cursor that is not a whole number and repeats an earlier page's, which marks the list partial |
| `rtr_audit_sessions` | `meta.pagination.total`, `meta.pagination.next`, `meta.pagination.offset` | service default | 500 | none | Pages of 500 sessions (limit), or the sessions left under the 500 cap when fewer. The first page sends no offset; each later page sends the previous page's meta.pagination.next (else meta.pagination.offset) when that is not a whole number; otherwise the previous page's offset (0 for the first page or after such a cursor) plus the number of sessions it returned. Without meta.pagination.total the walk runs to an empty page or the cap. A returned resource that is not a JSON object is dropped and marks the list partial. | meta.pagination.total sessions read; An empty page, which marks the list partial when fewer than meta.pagination.total sessions were read; 500 sessions read, which marks the list partial when meta.pagination.total is larger or, without a total, when the last page was full or carried a cursor that is not a whole number; A cursor that is not a whole number and repeats an earlier page's, which marks the list partial |
| `alerts` | `meta.pagination.after`, `meta.pagination.total` | service default | caller limit | none | Pages of 1000 alerts (limit), or the alerts left under alert_limit (default 2000, set from 1 through 10000) when fewer. The first page sends no after; each later page sends the previous page's meta.pagination.after. A returned resource that is not a JSON object is dropped and marks the list partial. | A page without meta.pagination.after, which marks the list partial when fewer than meta.pagination.total alerts were kept; alert_limit alerts read, an empty page, or a meta.pagination.after equal to the one the page was requested with, each of which marks the list partial unless meta.pagination.total is reported and that many alerts were kept |
| `contained_hosts`, `hosts` | `meta.pagination.total`, `meta.pagination.next`, `meta.pagination.offset` | service default | caller limit | none | Pages of 1000 hosts (limit), or the hosts left under host_limit (default 5000, set from 1 through 100000) when fewer. The first page sends no offset; each later page sends the previous page's meta.pagination.next (else meta.pagination.offset) when that is not a whole number; otherwise the previous page's offset (0 for the first page or after such a cursor) plus the number of hosts it returned. Without meta.pagination.total the walk runs to an empty page or the cap. A returned resource that is not a JSON object is dropped and marks the list partial. | meta.pagination.total hosts read; An empty page, which marks the list partial when fewer than meta.pagination.total hosts were read; host_limit hosts read, which marks the list partial when meta.pagination.total is larger or, without a total, when the last page was full or carried a cursor that is not a whole number; A cursor that is not a whole number and repeats an earlier page's, which marks the list partial |
| `discover_unmanaged_hosts` | `meta.pagination.after`, `meta.pagination.total` | service default | 100 | none | Pages of 100 hosts (limit), or the hosts left under the 100 cap when fewer. The first page sends no after; each later page sends the previous page's meta.pagination.after. A returned resource that is not a JSON object is dropped and marks the list partial. | A page without meta.pagination.after, which marks the list partial when fewer than meta.pagination.total hosts were kept; 100 hosts read, an empty page, or a meta.pagination.after equal to the one the page was requested with, each of which marks the list partial unless meta.pagination.total is reported and that many hosts were kept |
| `zero_trust_below_threshold` | `meta.pagination.after`, `meta.pagination.total` | service default | 1000 | none | Pages of 1000 results (limit), or the results left under the 1000 cap when fewer. The first page sends no after; each later page sends the previous page's meta.pagination.after. A returned resource that is not a JSON object is dropped and marks the list partial. | A page without meta.pagination.after, which marks the list partial when fewer than meta.pagination.total results were kept; 1000 results read, an empty page, or a meta.pagination.after equal to the one the page was requested with, each of which marks the list partial unless meta.pagination.total is reported and that many results were kept |
| `firewall_rule_group_ids`, `firewall_rule_ids` | `meta.pagination.total`, `meta.pagination.next`, `meta.pagination.offset` | service default | caller limit | none | Pages of 500 IDs (limit), or the IDs left under rule_limit (default 1000, set from 1 through 10000) when fewer. The first page sends no offset; each later page sends the previous page's meta.pagination.next (else meta.pagination.offset) when that is not a whole number; otherwise the previous page's offset (0 for the first page or after such a cursor) plus the number of IDs it returned. Without meta.pagination.total the walk runs to an empty page or the cap. The listed IDs that are non-blank strings or finite numbers are then looked up by ID, and fewer returned records than listed IDs marks the list partial. | meta.pagination.total IDs read; An empty page, which marks the list partial when fewer than meta.pagination.total IDs were read; rule_limit IDs read, which marks the list partial when meta.pagination.total is larger or, without a total, when the last page was full or carried a cursor that is not a whole number; A cursor that is not a whole number and repeats an earlier page's, which marks the list partial |

## Rate limits

| Scope | Documented limit | Retry headers | Retryable statuses | Policy |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CrowdStrike Security Inspector | Per API client: responses report the limit in X-Ratelimit-Limit and the requests left in X-Ratelimit-Remaining, which the collector does not read. | `X-Ratelimit-RetryAfter`, `Retry-After` | 429, 500-599 | Retry 429 and every status of 500 or above up to 4 times after the first attempt. The wait comes from X-Ratelimit-RetryAfter when the response carries it, else from Retry-After: a number above 1000000000000 is an epoch time in milliseconds, a number above 1000000000 an epoch time in seconds, any other non-negative number a count of seconds, and a value that is not a number is read as an HTTP date; an absent or unreadable value waits 250 milliseconds times 2 to the power of the number of earlier attempts. Every wait is clamped to 250 milliseconds through 15 seconds. A 401 discards the cached token and repeats the request once with a new one, and that repeat counts as one of the 4 retries. Requests that time out (30 seconds unless timeout_seconds or CS_TIMEOUT sets it) and network errors are not retried. |

### Primitive decision inputs

Every primitive is read from the named vendor surface or collector state before evidence lists are rendered or capped. Null and missing retain unavailable semantics; they are not empty inventories, false values, or zero counts.

| `CS-01` | `prevention_policies_readable` | Semantic owner: `CS-01.prevention_policies_readable`. Type/domain: boolean. Compared literal domain: true. Source/owner: CrowdStrike collector projection from `GET /policy/combined/prevention/v1` (prevention_policies). Completeness/sample semantics: The primitive is calculated from the uncapped collector state before any 25-item finding preview or export sample; list cardinalities therefore refer to every item the collector obtained. Null/missing meaning: the named source did not establish this primitive; null or absence cannot independently satisfy a passing rule. It feeds executable derived facts `cs_01_branch_01_matches`. Portable meaning: True when GET /policy/combined/prevention/v1 returned its prevention policy list without a refusal. |

Completeness/sample semantics: For CS-01, false when the prevention policy list is partial: it stopped at 5000 policies below meta.pagination.total (or, without a total, after a full last page or a cursor that is not a whole number), at an empty page before meta.pagination.total, or at a repeated cursor that is not a whole number, or a returned resource is not a JSON object. | `CS-22` | `sla_compliant_alert_count` | Semantic owner: `CS-22.sla_compliant_alert_count`. Type/domain: number. Source/owner: CrowdStrike collector projection from `POST /alerts/combined/alerts/v1` (alerts). Completeness/sample semantics: The primitive is calculated from the uncapped collector state before any 25-item finding preview or export sample; list cardinalities therefore refer to every item the collector obtained. Null/missing meaning: the named source did not establish this primitive; null or absence cannot independently satisfy a passing rule. It feeds executable derived facts `cs_22_branch_02_matches`, `cs_22_branch_03_matches`. Portable meaning: Number of returned alerts with a creation that count as within their SLA: the closed ones whose resolution time is at most their SLA hours, plus the ones that are not closed and whose age, the hours from creation to the assessment time (0 when negative), is at most their SLA hours. That second count is first reduced, never below 0, by the number of closed alerts with a creation and without a resolution time within their SLA whose status, trimmed, is not exactly the lowercase closed. An alert's creation is the first of its created_timestamp and timestamp that parses as a date. A value parses as a date when ECMAScript Date.parse reads its trimmed text, a finite number being read as its decimal text. An alert's SLA is 24 hours when its severity, read as a number (a finite number, or a string that is not blank and parses as one), is at least 90 or its severity_name trims and lowercases to critical, and 72 hours otherwise. An alert is closed when its status trims and lowercases to closed. A closed alert's resolution time is its seconds_to_resolved, read as a number (a finite number, or a string that is not blank and parses as one), divided by 3600 when that is above 0, else the hours from its creation to its updated_timestamp (0 when negative) when updated_timestamp parses as a date, else none. |

| `CS-09` | `policies_without_peripheral_restrictions` | Semantic owner: `CS-09.policies_without_peripheral_restrictions`. Type/domain: number. Compared literal domain: 0. Portable meaning: Number of assigned device control policies, as counted by assigned_device_control_policy_count that enforce none of the three peripheral restrictions. The three peripheral restrictions are Bluetooth, enforced when the details' bluetooth_settings.enforcement_mode reads as MONITOR_ENFORCE in any letter case and at least one JSON object in bluetooth_settings.classes has an action that does not read as FULL_ACCESS in any letter case, an absent action included; PCIe, enforced when usb_settings.pcie_enforcement_mode reads as MONITOR_ENFORCE in any letter case; and mass storage, enforced when the policy blocks mass storage. A policy blocks mass storage when its mass storage class has an action that reads as text other than FULL_ACCESS in any letter case. A policy's mass storage class is the last JSON object in its details' usb_settings.classes whose id, or class when id does not read as text, reads as MASS_STORAGE in any letter case. A policy's details are the last returned device control policy details record whose id and the policy's id read as the same text, or both do not read as text; a policy without details has none of their settings. A value reads as text when it is a string that is not blank, trimmed, or a finite number, in decimal. |

| `CS-12` | `policies_pinned_to_unsupported_build` | Semantic owner: `CS-12.policies_pinned_to_unsupported_build`. Type/domain: number. Compared literal domain: 0. Source/owner: CrowdStrike collector projection from `GET /policy/combined/sensor-update/v2` (sensor_update_policies), `GET /policy/combined/sensor-update-builds/v1` (sensor_update_builds). Portable meaning: Number of assigned sensor update policies, as counted by assigned_sensor_update_policy_count with a pinned judged build whose build number is not among the supported build numbers of the policy's catalog, counted only when that catalog was read without a refusal. A policy's judged builds are its settings.build, whether or not it reads as text, plus the build of each JSON object in its settings.variants array whose build reads as text. A value reads as text when it is a string that is not blank, trimmed, or a finite number, in decimal. A build that does not read as text turns updates off. Otherwise, trimmed and lowercased, it auto-updates when one of its pipe-separated segments is n, n-1, or n-2; else it is pinned when it starts with a run of digits, or with two runs of digits each followed by a dot and then a run of digits (as in 7.10.16004), and that last run ends the build or is followed by a pipe, the run being its build number; else its form is unknown. A policy's platform is its platform_name read as text and lowercased, and its catalog is the GET /policy/combined/sensor-update-builds/v1 read for that platform. A catalog's supported build numbers come from its returned build records whose build, trimmed and lowercased, has a pipe-separated segment of n, n-1, or n-2: the leading run of digits of that build, else the trailing run of digits of the record's sensor_version, both read as text; a record with neither offers none. |

A client's scopes are the entries of its scopes array. A JSON object scope's id is its id, else name, else scope, read as text; its group is its group read as text, else the group parsed from its id; and its action is its action read as text, else the action parsed from its id. Any other scope's id is the scope read as text, with its group and action parsed from that id. Text made of a group without a colon, a colon, and an action of letters, underscores, and hyphens, with optional whitespace around each, parses to that group and action; other text parses to the whole text as the group and no action; a scope whose id does not read as text parses to neither. A scope is a sensitive write scope when its action contains write or admin in any letter case, and its group or its id, trimmed, lowercased, and with every run of whitespace or underscores turned into one hyphen, contains prevention, response, sensor-update, device-control, firewall, user-management, api-client, host, exclusion, identity-protection, alert, detect, or incident. |

### Ordered decision rules

Rules are evaluated from lowest order number to highest. The first matching condition determines the finding status; later rules are not evaluated.

A `matches` condition performs a regular-expression search; anchors are required for whole-value matching, and an `i` flag requests case-insensitive matching. A `ratio` condition divides the numerator by the denominator, applies the declared scale, and rounds to the declared decimal places by choosing the nearest value with exact half cases rounded toward positive infinity; a zero, null, or missing denominator does not match.

## Collection states

| State | Required rendering |
|---|---|
| complete | complete: proven API exhaustion or a successful single-object read. |
| truncated | truncated: preserve seen and total when available plus the exact stop reason. |
| unreadable | unreadable: render data and counts as null and retain a scrubbed error envelope. |
| denied | denied: render null evidence with the endpoint and HTTP status, never an empty inventory. |
| not requested | not_requested: identify the unreadable parent dependency and do not invent an HTTP status. |
| not configured | not_configured: identify the absent optional feature or credential without treating it as compliant. |

## Integration-specific scrubbing

Shared contract version: 1.1.

Projection stage: Every response passes the data redaction pass when it is read, which rewrites remembered credential values in raw and encoded forms, credential carriers, and token-shaped text in every string. Alerts and RTR sessions are then exported projected to their declared snapshot fields, exclusions are exported after the exclusion text pass rewrites their cl_regex, ifn_regex, value, name, description, and comment, and every other dataset is exported as read.

Sensitive fields and values: client_secret, access_token, Authorization

Credential formats: CrowdStrike Falcon API client ID and client secret, exchanged at /oauth2/token for a bearer token, CrowdStrike Falcon OAuth2 bearer access token

Reviewed benign exceptions: Stable non-secret resource identifiers and public documentation URLs remain visible unless carried in a credential field.

Integration-specific rules:

- Withhold undocumented error bodies; retain only status, media type, byte length, and allowlisted vendor error codes.
- Remove URL user information, queries, and fragments from evidence and reject off-origin pagination links.
- Unavailable counts, arrays, maps, and negative flags are null rather than fabricated empty values.

Projected fields by surface:

| Surface | Allowed fields |
|---|---|
| `prevention_policies` | `id`, `name`, `enabled`, `platform_default`, `groups`, `platform_name`, `prevention_settings.settings.id`, `prevention_settings.settings.value.enabled`, `prevention_settings.settings.value.detection`, `prevention_settings.settings.value.prevention` |

### Record schemas

#### finding

- `id`
- `title`
- `severity`
- `status`
- `summary`
- `evidence`
- `framework mappings`

#### collection_marker

- `collected`
- `status`
- `endpoint`
- `error`

#### bundle_result

- `outputDir`
- `zipPath`
- `fileCount`
- `findingCount`
- `errorCount`

#### assessment

- `title or category`
- `summary`
- `findings`
- `errors when collection was partial`

#### pagination_state

- `items or rows seen`
- `reported total when available`
- `pages`
- `truncated`
- `stop reason`

JSON formatting: UTF-8 JSON with two-space indentation and a trailing newline.

Overwrite policy: Allocate {cloud, else the base_url host}[-{member_cid}]-audit-bundle, lowercased with every run of characters outside a-z, 0-9, dot, underscore, and hyphen replaced by one hyphen, trimmed of leading and trailing hyphens, and cut to 80 characters (crowdstrike when nothing is left), or the first of -2 through -9 whose directory and zip are both free, and fail rather than overwrite.

Path safety: Resolve beneath the configured output root and reject traversal, unsafe parents, files, and symbolic-link escapes.

Archive pairing: Write a sibling zip named from the exact allocated bundle-directory path plus .zip.
